ParlaMint-AT_2006-03-02-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00140_d7e2717	The meeting was opened. I should like to ask the ladies and gentlemen to rise from their seats. [[All present rise from their places.]] [[09:00:22]] Griefing
ParlaMint-AT_2006-03-02-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00140_d7e2748	High house! Ladies and gentlemen! We've just reached the news that Mag. Leopold Gratz died today. Leopold Gratz has been closely connected to this house since 1953. He started as the club secretary of the Social Democratic Parliamentary Group, was then elected to the Federal Council by the Vienna Landtag in 1963, and in 1966 he changed to the National Council. From 1970 to 1971 he was the Federal Minister of Education, then Klubobmann became the Social Democratic Group of this House, in 1973 became Mayor of the City of Vienna, and in 1984 he again became Federal Minister for Foreign Affairs. In 1986 he was sitting here on this chair and was an unbelievably respected President of the National Council. As a young Member, I was under his chairmanship. Again and again his humanity, his joke, his humor – he was really a gentleman – impressed me. Leopold Gratz was a great servant of the Republic. His commitment was comprehensive and his subject matter as well; the wide range of his activity proves this. Leopold Gratz distinguished fine humor and humanity. He was a great Austrian. I ask you to remember at this minute of his. Our compassion also applies to his great family, his grandchildren. I've been in contact with him until the last time. We mourn for him. [[Those present persist for some time in dumb mourning.]] Thank you.

The ORF is probably the best public service station with the highest power ratios across Europe. You can convince yourself by the radio. Every day, “Austria 1” is heard on average by a quarter of a million Austrians, “Austria Regional” by one million, and “Austria 3” by another million. And all these channels also have information services. In Television had an average of nearly one and a half million spectators last year. “Time in Figure 2” has about half a million and “Time in Figure 1” about 1.3 to 1.4 million. The more interesting is that even more selective is seen: People don't sit in front of the electronic altar every night, but if something happens, they'll be there. Otherwise, you can see entertainment on any channel, but if something is going on – you have noticed it in the tsunami, in the death of the Pope, in the terrorist attacks in London or in the great documentation of Hugo Portisch – then the quota increases to 1.5 to 1.7 million spectators. ParlaMint-AT_2006-03-02-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00140_d7e3389	Appreciate Mr. Federal Chancellor! Ladies and gentlemen. My question is: 135/M "What measures has the Federal Government taken in recent months to combat unemployment effectively?"
ParlaMint-AT_2006-03-02-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00140_d7e3411	Federal Chancellor, please.
ParlaMint-AT_2006-03-02-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00140_d7e3426	Deputies! High house! A year ago, we launched the largest tax reform in the Second Republic, whose impact will be fully effective in this year, especially for companies with regard to corporate tax. This effect of tax reform alone accounts for about half a percent of additional economic growth and explains why we are about half this percent above the growth of the euro area. This has, I think, been a very important step. Secondly, we have concluded regional growth agreements with all the federal states, bringing in a total of about 1.2 billion additional money to the regions, which ultimately releases about 3 billion of investment. Then, in autumn, we decided on an additional package for the labour market service of about €300 million, which is now beginning to work. Some 60 000 people are expected to receive additional qualifications in this year. In total, € 1 billion more money for active labour market promotion is available this year than in 1999.

ParlaMint-AT_2006-03-02-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00140_d7e7378	Mr President. High house! My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! In Austria, there are some policy areas that could be held out of the party dispute. This is the question of animal protection, anti-atomic policy and genetic engineering – not least because there is a broad consensus in this regard also in Austrian society, which has manifested itself in the question of genetic engineering also in the success of the genetic engineering people's desire. In the fight against genetic engineering, there are five essential strategies that can be driven here. The first is that national prohibition regulations prohibit the introduction of genetically modified organisms. This is a strategy that is good, which is also being pursued. However, it is also a strategy which is under massive attack and which at least falls or threatens to function because the European Commission is trying to eliminate it. A second possibility is the question of co-existence, that we introduce rules of distance between organic and conventional agriculture and agriculture, where genetic engineering is used. This is a strategy where there will now also be a Commission conference in Vienna in April, which will definitely also be pursued. As a third party, there is the possibility of allowing or even preventing de facto genetic engineering by strict liability rules. I'm saying you could do something more. In this respect, we have already put forward proposals to this end. A fourth strategy is labelling. At European level, we have the labelling regulation, which is all right, but too little to do with it. There is also a request from the Greens, which my colleagues will address even more precisely, which we support in principle, because, of course, if we want to give the consumer the power to decide whether or not genetic engineering is used in agriculture, then he must also know whether this happens, that is, if genetic engineering is used in meat production, for example, then For example, this should be recognizable in the purchase of meat, because otherwise the consumer will support something that he does not want to support. The fifth option is that of the free zones free of genetic engineering, a variant which is accepted on a voluntary basis, but which could not yet be established successfully by law in Europe. We are introducing a new initiative, a new proposal, and a proposal that we have already tabled to the Committee on Agriculture, which I am allowed to explain briefly. That is the motion for a resolution tabled by Mr Krainer, Mr Maier, Mr Gaßner. I should like to explain the essential key points that the Austrian Federal Government is being called upon to introduce, in agreement with the national governments, a ban on the release and release of GMOs into the national parks of Austria, in nuclear zones and also protection zones, to keep the national parks free of genetic engineering and that the Austrian Federal Government should also support the countries in their efforts to provide, within their competence, the know-how that exists at the federal level, also to the federal states, because of the fact that small federal states of course cannot build up such centres of competence as a large federal country or even the Republic. The strategy behind this is that we believe that, in order to create GMO-free zones – Upper Austria has tried to make the whole of the federal state fail – it is not going the way to start with the impossible, but it is starting where, in principle, no one can say, the European Commission cannot say no, in the case of the national parks that have the highest level of protection, where there is already a restriction on the possibilities of agriculture in the peripheral zones and in the protection zones. This would be a further restriction to be GMO-free also in the protection zones and in the peripheral zones. This is the first step, because we believe that this is also most easily enforceable by law. Of course, we are already thinking of the next step, with Natura 2000 projects, with nature parks, with bird reserves and so on, in order not to do what Oberösterreich has done, to begin with the impossible, and then to achieve nothing, but we want to go another and also an offensive path and not a defensive one. We have heard kind words from the government parties, especially from the liberties in the committee. The conversations we invited, where we also sent e-mails, were very disappointing, however, we did not receive an answer. It would be very sad if the ÖVP left the way of national consensus in genetic engineering, which the four parties wanted to go together. On the basis of your behaviour, we reserve the right to consent to your request, which does not contain anything wrong, but, of course, is very dirty and not concrete enough compared to other requests. We request our consent here. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2006-03-02-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00140_d7e23356	Madam President. Mr. State Secretary! High house! This agenda item deals with three per­ception reports with seven audit results, and I hope you understand that I am addressing the Austro-Retirement Retirement Report in particular with regard to the budgetary importance and also with regard to topicality. I would like to point out that over the years, agreements have just been concluded by the ÖBB with the services representatives on behalf of third parties, namely at the expense of the federal government or at the expense of taxpayers. I would like to point out that, for example, at the end of 1995, all employees were given the opportunity to switch to the unannounceable official scheme at the ÖBB, which led to the use of 3 100 staff by the end of 2002 and that now the ratio of ASVG staff to unannounceable staff is 12 : 88; originally, the ratio was 19 : 81. It was true that the ÖBB also introduced a pension fund for all new entrants from 1995 onwards, which is certainly positive. The ÖBB has also committed itself to paying 1.5 percent of the gross debt. At the same time, however, a minimum yield of 5 percent has been set. This is remarkable, considering that the ÖBB has no control over this minimum yield or on the pension fund, so that even in the case of a full expansion from 2033 onwards, up to €95 million would have been incurred for the ÖBB in costs. As a success of the Court of Auditors, I can point out that with 31. On 1 March 2004 this pension fund was cancelled for new entrants, but this pension fund is still up to date for officials from the 1995 to March 2004. In this context, the Court of Auditors regrets the failure to terminate the minimum return and, at the same time, the opening of this pension fund model for all others, i.e. for all new entrants, to resist the staff representation. Another point on what I have just said was that taxpayers' agreements have been taxed, is an example of the fact that, as of 1995, new entrants have also been developed a cost-saving model of working time, which is why the work plans at ÖBB were geared to 40 hours per week. It has been noted without much discussion with the owner that it would be in line with the developments in working time if one demanded of new entrants not 40 hours of the week but 38.5 hours of the week. They have been offered 1.5 hours per week, these hours per week are paid up with the 50 per cent overtime supplement and that this benefit or the amount will be paid annually. The consequence of this cost-saving model of working time would be an annual effort for the ÖBB of €26 million from 2033 on, and at the same time a reserve requirement of €1 billion. A point in which the Court of Auditors feels confirmed, too, because this led to the announcement of this savings model in September 2004. The timetable has been corrected and the services previously saved have been compensated for, respectively, hours. An important point is – and it is still up-to-date, and I believe that Mr Amon will know this too – that the Railway Change Act 1997 has raised the average additional fee rate of 10 per cent to 15 per cent, which is particularly relevant for the pension, from 2003 to 2020. Considering that the actual average additional charges in 2002 amounted to 10,42 percent, it is seen that, after no professional group reaches this 15 percent, this will result in a massive additional burden on the federal government, which is why the Court of Auditors is going to distance itself from this flat-rate average rate. This is not the last reason, since this particularly heavy worker - especially today the heavy worker scheme has been decided - who actually have higher additional charges. However, they receive only 10 per cent of the pension, while people who are on the inside who have lower additional fees also have the possibility to receive a 10% extra charge average rate on their pension. This is a point which is also anachronistic and socially unbearable from social concerns, or also from the point of view of the availability of all salary data. Another point that has also been added is the fact that one has also made the general adversarial allowance a part of the salary and has therefore increased the pension base by 5 percent in 2002. It is also a measure that has a billion-height impact on the Federal Budget. I think it would be worth remembering that the provisions of the 1997 Railway Law Change Act, in which it is stated that the Federal Government has to make payments only if they are comprehensible, should be reviewed accordingly. It is not surprising, therefore, to look at this measure, that the pension gap has risen more and more in recent years, and that in In 2004, the pension gap between paid amounts and paid pensions has already amounted to €1.2 billion. It should also be noted that the average age of the ÖBB in 2002 is still 52.2 years, and in 2005 it is 52.8 years. This means that ÖBB staff now have less contribution times by 8.7 years than, for example, an ASVG staff member, with reference to the year 2005. It has certainly been – and this is positive to say – also taken by the Federal Railway Pension Act in the case of ÖBB measures which have gone towards harmonisation with officials and with the ASVG staff and which have actually affected or reduced these special benefits, but it should also be noted that the pension gap continues to rise. It should be considered – I would like to mention this in particular because the public health services are at my heart, and I know that they provide an excellent service, because I also know that the public health service providers pay a higher pension contribution, pay a higher pension security contribution, that they have a special specificity of the service – it should be considered whether the special benefits that are still available in this area are portable, whether they are eligible and whether they should not be sought to bring about harmonisation between officials, ASVG staff and public health officials. [[Congratulations with the liberals and representatives of the ÖVP.]] In this context, I would just like to point out that it may be necessary to remember whether the 83 per cent basis of assessment is still eligible for funding, even with regard to the supplementary rate, which has been included in the pension base. It is also necessary to consider whether it is actually good or financial that an ÖBB employee with 83 percent maximum basis of assessment and the required waiting period of 60 months at the end of the extension can retire at no later than 58 years without deductions. I would like to point out, for example, that this is only the case with an official or ASVG staff only if he retires from sickness due to illness, but in that case he also has to pay corresponding reductions. It will also be necessary to consider, when dealing with this issue of retirement at ÖBB, that there are still rules in the field of public health which motivate people to retire early, namely in the way that officials, for example, are generally taken into account in ASVG periods, but that ASVG officials are only counted as ASVG times if they retire due to illness. If you look at how many people are sick, how many sick days there are, of course, many things are also motivated by the fact that people need sickness-related retirement for the calculation of the ASVG times. There is also something to consider in connection with the anniversary grants, where it is noted that there is a 40-year anniversary of service, but that is generally paid out at the ÖBB after 35 years. It was the same with the officials, but was abolished, so that only in special cases after 35 years the anniversary allowance is paid. However, this point is still striking with the ÖBB. ParlaMint-AT_2006-03-02-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00140_d7e26904	Mr Neudeck, you really challenge me with your calls, especially with regard to Mr Praschak, whom I certainly knew better than you. On Norway and hence on the subject. The amendment to the agreement we are now considering essentially concerns taxation of intra-group cross-border profit distributions. In concrete terms, in the case of a transfer from a Contracting State to the other Contracting State, the tax law of the former State of residence shall be maintained on the silent reserves for five years. The aim is to prevent companies from moving their headquarters only for tax protection in the short term. Unquestionably, and this is properly maintained, this agreement does not, of course, fall within the scope of EU law. The cover of the government template, however, correctly states that this – I quote – is to create “the same fiscal competitive situation as in relation to the EU countries”. – This is an opportunity to focus briefly on this issue, namely the issue of the harmonisation of taxes in the European Union. Unquestionably – and this is undisputed – harmonisation in the field of indirect taxation has made it possible to create the single internal market for goods at all. On the other hand, as far as direct taxation is concerned, the almost unhindered tax dumping has been taken care of, in fact, by some even speaking – and by far not only of the new accession countries. In the interests of fair competition and thus increasing the EU's acceptance of the population, we should pay high attention to this issue of the harmonisation of direct taxation. We also had the opportunity to discuss this issue with representatives of the European Parliament's Economic and Monetary Committee here in this House. I believe, and this is particularly important, that we should not confine ourselves to the discussion of uniform basis of assessment, but in particular to the discussion on the introduction of minimum tax rates in the field of direct taxation, particularly in the field of corporate taxation. Only then can we be in line with what is indicated in the front page of the current government template by the formulation of “equal fiscal competitive situation”. We shall, of course, give our approval to this revision of the agreement.

ParlaMint-AT_2006-03-02-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00140_d7e27016	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! My venerations Ladies and gentlemen! In 1999, the highly successful flexibilisation clause was introduced. The aim of this flexi clause was and is to provide services with more flexibility and responsibility in resource management, thereby contributing to a more efficient and effective use of resources. I believe that this is an important objective, which we all call for, desired and necessary. In many areas, we often argue about rather, I believe, supposed things that by far do not have these effects on the budget. We should and should be able and able to strengthen this possibility. This Flexi clause was always limited in time, ultimately until 31. December of this year to evaluate the advantages and disadvantages. In view of the far-reaching results and the efforts to modernise and flexibility of budgetary rights, it is useful to continue the current provisions on the Flexi clause. The savings actually made are a good argument for this. The 2006 budgetary reform clearly sets out the objectives, using tax money efficiently, reducing public expenditure, introducing a global budget, a stability-oriented financial policy, gender budgeting and focusing on citizens' performance. And these ambitious goals can only be achieved by motivated employees. In turn, this so-called Flexi clause is used because results-oriented work, more self-reliance, performance premiums, promotion of own ideas in the sense of more innovation in public administration are also possible in terms of administrative reform. They form the basis of a budget management with house mind. It should not be the case that at the end of a budget year, at the end of the year, it is sometimes difficult to think whether the last funds can still be used in any way, because otherwise they will be lost, but it is much better to be able to use budget resources, which can reasonably be saved, at least partially for their own areas, so that efficiency is the consequence. This project of budgetary reform, the global budget or the effective management includes: A service receives the allocated global budget, which the service can freely have and within which it can also rework according to its requirements. The principles of budgetary management such as efficiency, transparency and, as far as possible, faithful presentation of the financial situation – and not artificially bloated budgets – are important. They can be implemented in the best possible way and allow modern business management, as is natural in private companies, in successful industrial enterprises. Such a defined global budget makes sense because it links the results and resource responsibility in a targeted way. It's going to be... to implement this modern budget management also in the Austrian budget culture. Mr State Secretary, on the part of the Austrian People's Party, on the part of the government parties, we very much agree with this law. On this occasion I would also like to thank the officials of your home for the very good, very professional and forward-looking proposals that the budget section has made. It should be recognized what officials are doing in your home; not only can you point your finger at them, but you can also praise them when it is appropriate.

ParlaMint-AT_2006-03-02-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00140_d7e27447	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! Dear ladies and gentlemen! High house! This flexibilisation clause, which is here today, is a real success story, and I therefore think it is very wise that the time-limit is lifted. In the short term, the three main reasons for this: The idea behind it is very simple: The officials involved are no longer forced into a rigid budget, but a performance agreement is concluded with them. So officials are no longer required to do what they have to do, but instead they agree a goal with their Federal Minister. The way there, however, can be determined by the officials themselves. As simple as that may be, ladies and gentlemen, it is a real revolution. Second point: This model helps save costs, because the principle of this flexi clause is: Under the rule, the respective area must not cost the State more than in the previous year. Whether the officials achieve it, that they spend less or take more, that's their business. I am already at the third point in this success story: The officials like to do this. And that is a point I think is very, very important personally. Officials can simply do more with this Flexi clause, bring in their experiences and implement their ideas. This new form of administration is worth it both for the State and for the officials. But I believe that this is primarily not just about the money, but rather about forcing officials to be forced to do so in addition to powerful citizens.

Ulrike Lunacek, colleagues on a federal law with which the Name Change Act, BGBl. No. 195/1988, amended (774/A)
ParlaMint-AT_2006-03-02-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00140_d7e28078	We now reach the 17th. Agenda item. We are going into the debate. Mrs Mag. Lunacek as an applicant has the first word message. 5 minutes welcome. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2006-03-02-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00140_d7e28106	Ladies and gentlemen! Mr President. At this first reading, we are talking about an application for a change of law, which may seem a bit strange to some of you, because why should we still amend the Name Change Act? Our request goes in the direction that the first first name should not correspond to the applicant’s/applicant’s gender. In the Austrian law, the first name must correspond to the gender determined at birth. Some of you may have wondered that in other countries it is different than with us, for example, that forenames that are clearly associated with women's sex, such as Andrea in Italy, are men's names – for example – or that there are forenames in some countries, especially in the Anglican region, which are both male and female, which can have both a man and a woman. Jamie, for example, is one of them. In English it is often even so that by name it is not clear which sex you have. There are first names like April or January, where you wonder why, but anyway, that is possible. – This is not possible in Austria. What's the problem now? – The problem is that there are people – even if it is perhaps incomprehensible to some of us, but there is! – which occur in the wrong sex from birth or from the time they begin to think, so to speak, in the wrong body, where the birth book says that is a boy. But this bub always feels that he's actually a girl. He feels more comfortable with girls. He prefers to do things that are attributed to the girl, he'd rather be a girl. There are some of these people – not so few at all, perhaps they have already met some of you – who simply want to live when they are grown up in the sex in which they feel. Some people like to make fun of it. I don't think that's a point you're supposed to make fun of. There is also the possibility of having a sex-adapted operation done. According to Austrian law, it is only then that one's first name in the public documents – e.g. in the driving licence or in the passport – is only to this new Gender can be adjusted if you have already undergone the whole process, starting from hormone therapy to psychological care to gender-transformation surgery, and if you finally confirm a doctor or a doctor: Now you're also biological in the opposite sex! So with the secondary body characteristics, as far as it's going to stop. Only then can we make this change. But there are people who do not want to do this operation at all, who simply want to live between the sexes, who simply want to be in clothing, in the habitat and in the name of the opposite sex, and who do not want to make this process of adaptation, which often lasts for years. It is not possible for these people to adopt the first name of the opposite sex in everyday life – a gender-neutral name already. Imagine – and that's not just something for the carnival, he's over now – a man who has the feeling that he's a woman goes through a woman's clothes. – That’s also what you need to do in this process before surgery – this daily test. He drives by car, has police control, sits as a woman in the car, and then has to show the driver's license, which contains a male first name. – Skill You're imagining the police's reaction? That's not easy. Or an interview: One wants to apply for a job, according to the book of birth, according to document a woman, but acts as a man and feels as a man. Getting a job is very difficult in this situation. What it is about us Greens – and there are also several organisations that want to do so – is simply to allow in Austria the possibility that the first name does not correspond to the biological gender of the applicant or the applicant – that is to say, what is in the birth book. That's something that doesn't cost anything, or any other trouble. It would help only some people to live in everyday life more recognized and without fears. The current issue of the “Falter” includes a contribution about an insurance mathematicalian, a woman living as a man who is still a biological man. She says she feels like a cancer disease that has to explain at every ID check that she has cancer because she has to say at every ID check, in her passport she still says that she is a man, but she feels and lives as a woman. In the simplest case, she has to expect evil looks, in the worst case she has to live with consequences in the workplace, in everyday life, where he or she lives. I very much hope that it is possible to have an objective debate on this request and indeed to facilitate these people – a relief that exists in other countries, because simply the obligation to identify with the sex by the first name is not there. This would make life easier for individuals, for some people. I think we're here in the high house, too. It doesn't cost anything, but it brings a few things. I hope that you will also have the approval of the government parties. Thank you.

Why? – The question is justified. On the one hand, there is a criminal provision which has even been intensified with the novel to the postal law: Who's up to 30. In June 2006, ÖNORM does not apply the locker system in 32,5 x 3 cm format, subject to a penalty penalty of € 30 000. Now, of course, you can say that the individual does not meet it, it meets the housing owners community. Ladies and gentlemen! That's not true either. In the law one has forgotten to define what can be understood as a building. Since one has assumed that under buildings a building with more than four housing units can be understood, and has not questioned why, we are facing the situation today that one and two-family houses are also included. I have looked at comparable criminal provisions in other laws. In the Food Act – or now the Food Safety and Consumer Protection Act – we have provisions that say that there is a maximum penalty of about €7,200 for someone who markets depraved foods – that is, smelling foods. If someone transmits data illegally under the Data Protection Act, the maximum penalty is about €7,200. However, if someone does not apply this standard locker, the maximum penalty is €30 000. Why is that? Will this force the competition after the Supreme Court in Austria had just before – before 2003 – founded the delivery practice with the safe delivery? Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! I do not see this regulation in this form, nor can I accept it on behalf of my group. There has been a very strong public discussion, especially on these standard lockers in format 32,5 x 3 cm. I live in a settlement where the locker system is accessible to all – even for private service providers – in the format 27.5 x 2.9 cm. Everyone has access to this locker. According to the provisions of this law, we must tear out our locker system and re-install it at the property limit. This is the next problem: It is currently no longer permitted for new buildings in a settlement to integrate locks directly into the house area. On the basis of the wording of this law, you have to go to the property border, which in turn means: If you live on the third floor of a house and have always been used to going down in the bathrobe in the early morning and pick up the paper, this will not be possible anymore, because you will have to go 100 or 200 meters until you reach the factory at the property border. My venerations Ladies and gentlemen, I really wonder: What did the gentlemen think about in the Ministry of Transport? Another example, because, in my opinion, my Salzburg colleague Peter Haubner speaks: There is the Salzburg Altstadterhaltungsgesetz. This prohibits certain structural measures. ==References== According to § 14 of the Postal Act, it will not be possible to install such locks in the area of the protection zone or even in listed houses. The list of all these reasons can be continued, in particular with regard to the safety of these lockers, where one can intervene. You can try it all out: You can just drive in by hand and get the mail out. I wonder where the security remains at the time of delivery! I got a lot of letters from people affected. I quote you from one: Ladies and gentlemen! Please help us and try to convince your colleagues of the other political groups, of which there are hopefully some reasonable ones, that this law is a disgrace. - Quote. Ladies and gentlemen! I hope there are some reasonable ones in your circle so that we can change this § 14 together.
